I mean, I still show up for things, like the Sunday routines with my wife, but I don’t really feel interested in them anymore. Even good things at work just kind of pass by.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods in the past year and seven months where you felt extremely down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had low days, sure. I’m not sure I’d call them “extremely depressed.” It’s kind of hard to sort out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I don’t really get that feeling anymore… happiness, or closeness. My wife still tries with our old Sunday routines, and if something goes well at work, I know it should feel good, but it just doesn’t really land.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year and seven months where you felt so down that it was difficult to function?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there’ve been times. At work I’ve had to hand off floor stuff I used to just handle myself. And there was an interview with a regional director where I… I lost it a bit and walked out.\n\nAt home, I’m there, but I’m not really… in it. My wife feels that, I think. It’s been hard on us.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of two weeks or more where you felt depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. It’s been… low a lot, but I don’t really track it that way. Two weeks straight, most of the day… I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest or pleasure in almost all activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, I guess. It’s not like I’m sad every second, but the good stuff doesn’t really feel like anything. My wife tries to get me into our old Sunday things, and even when work goes well, I know I should feel something… I just don’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of two weeks or more where you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I’ve had stretches where I felt pretty low, but whether it was nearly every day for two weeks… I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Some days it feels that way, but I couldn’t say it’s most of the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of two weeks or more where you felt depressed for most of every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know I’ve had bad stretches, but saying it was most of every day for two weeks… I don’t know if I can say that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed for most of the day, most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I have days where I’m really down, but most of the day, most days… I don’t know if I’d put it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in things most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s less like I’m crying all day and more like I just don’t really feel happy anymore. Even when my wife tries to get me to do our old Sunday stuff, or something goes well at work, it just doesn’t… land. I don’t feel close to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a clear period where you felt depressed that started and stopped, rather than feeling this way all the time since the accident?\n\nPatient: Not really a clear start and stop, no. It’s been kind of there since the accident… the retrofit accident. That was, what, about a year and seven months ago now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
